No gate bypass: step 5 + guardrails still require branch, self-check, PR and Reviewer-only merge, and state the skill never replaces AGENTS.md or weakens a test layer @443339f: python3 scripts/teamwork.py links -> # issues: 0; skill list -> S-0001 ceo-audit-close [shared] owner=ceo file=ok; identity --audit --range main..HEAD -> 2 commits, 0 problems (author==committer==CEO <ceo@agents.nidvue.com> == git config); diff main...HEAD is one file, .agents/skills/ceo-audit-close/SKILL.md
- findings history: BLOCKING - the failure-mode row in .agents/skills/ceo-audit-close/SKILL.md ("manifest head != `git rev-parse HEAD` -> the export predates the merge; regenerate") describes a condition that can never be satisfied, so the skill tells every close-out to loop forever. `ledger export` records `git.head = rev-parse HEAD` at export time (engine.py:2504-2515) and writes the manifest, which is then committed in a later commit (step 5 of the same skill), so the recorded head is always an ancestor of the commit that carries the manifest. Evidence from this repository: the commit that first added the manifest, 384df6a (parent a531fb8), carries `docs/Team/AuditManifest.json` with `git.head = a531fb8` = its own parent; today the committed manifest says `git.head = 394c19b` while `git rev-parse main` = 2297adc, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor 394c19b main` succeeds. Following the row, the CEO must "regenerate", which reproduces the same one-commit offset on every attempt - an unsatisfiable gate in a skill whose whole value is that its steps are exact. `audit` does not check this either (engine.py:6467-6484 only compares the recorded ledger record count against the current count), so the row is also not engine-derived behaviour. Fix: state the invariant that actually holds - the manifest `git.head` must be the reviewed milestone merge and an ancestor of the current HEAD (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <manifest.git.head> HEAD`), never equality - and drop "regenerate" or scope it to "the export predates the milestone merge" with that ancestor test. Re-check step 4/6 wording at the same time so "export last" and this check do not contradict each other. NON-BLOCKING - `SKILL.md` step 2 uses an undefined `<script>` placeholder (`<script> ledger verify`); the rest of the repository's agent-facing commands spell it `python3 $S ...` (SKILL.md:606-609) or `teamwork.py ...`. Pick one so the copied commands run. So one repro for bug A still satisfies every sibling bug in the area, which is exactly what the commit message claims is fixed ("One repro for one bug must not satisfy a sibling bug in the same area") and what AGENTS.md requires ("a defect is proved before it is fixed"). The new test test_area_level_repro_covers_a_task_without_an_id_link pins the loophole as intended behaviour. Minimal fix options: (a) refuse `tests repro` on a record whose area holds a `kind: bug` task unless the record names the task, or make `tests add` require `--task` when such a task exists; (b) if genuinely-legacy records must keep working, mark them explicitly (e.g. `legacy: true`) instead of inferring legacy from an empty field; either way document the rule in references/Evidence.md and references/Testing.md so the documented flow produces a task-linked record. AGENTS.md "Documentation and wiki updates are complete" applies. Why it matters: AGENTS.md is the file every agent is told to obey and it self-describes the cap as hard, so after this merge an agent following the rulebook throttles to 10 while the ledger says 64 - the exact failure mode the decision was written to fix. Expected: either raise the four doc lines in the same change-set, or record the decision as status: proposed and open the doc task. Actual: accepted decision + unchanged hard rule. The string does exist on this host (48 hits under ~/.pi/agent/sessions/--home-pingz-Code-Nidvue-Teamwork--/), but that is machine-local session text, not a reproducible measurement: it names no revision, no date and no artifact, and the "before -> TOTAL 2638" half cannot be re-run now that Settings.json already holds 64. - findings history: Two defects, both proved by re-runnable mutation in an archive copy of 756ad8d. (1) THE CHARTER SURFACE IS UNGUARDED, contrary to D-20261020-CEO consequences. docs/Team/Decisions.md:586 claims "all 15 role charters state the line from ROLE_PARENT" and "tests/unit/test_reporting_hierarchy.py pins the docs to the constant". tests/unit/test_reporting_hierarchy.py never reads roles/*.md (only templates/AGENTS.md:91 and references/Roles.md:102). Proof: in a /tmp archive of 756ad8d, sed roles/developer.md line 8 "**CTO.** You escalate" -> "**CEO.** You escalate" then `pytest tests/unit/test_reporting_hierarchy.py -q` -> 9 passed. Same for `Manages:` lines: architect.md:11 "Manages: Developer, Tester." -> "Manages: Tester." (now contradicting roles/cto.md:11 "Manages: ... Developer ...") -> 9 passed. A charter can therefore silently contradict the tree the whole design rests on, which is exactly the drift the decision record says is impossible. The charter surface is decorative where it matters most, because the charter is what the agent actually reads at startup. (2) THE 14 CHARTERS THIS PR EDITS CONTAIN A SELF-CONTRADICTORY SENTENCE. All 14 new "## Reports to" sections reuse one template: "**<Parent>.** You escalate up that line, not to the CEO, unless it is a red line, a goal change, or a decision that binds the whole team." When the parent IS the CEO that reads "**CEO.** You escalate up that line, not to the CEO, unless ...". Affected: roles/cto.md:8, roles/coo.md:8, roles/cfo.md:8, roles/product-manager.md:8, roles/reviewer.md:45, roles/security-auditor.md:8 (6 of 14). Proof: `grep -n "^\*\*CEO\.\*\* You escalate up that line, not to the CEO" roles/*.md` -> 6 hits. This is the whole deliverable failing its own honesty requirement at the surface the reader trusts most; the test does not catch it because the charters are untested (defect 1). Also wrong but not blocking: docs/Team/Decisions.md:580,581,584 justify the change with "13 of 15 roles named the CEO". Measured on main: references/Roles.md has 15 role rows, 14 name CEO, only 6 name CEO alone (the other 8 are "CEO, <peer>"); templates/AGENTS.md main table has 12 role rows, 12 name CEO. Neither surface yields 13. The direction of the argument holds (and the sole-CEO count did drop 6 -> 6 with the co-parents collapsed to one, and names-CEO 14 -> 6), but a number in a decision record that no grep on main reproduces is the kind of unsourced claim the anti-hallucination rule forbids. Restate it as "14 of 15 rows named the CEO, 8 of them with two managers". - findings history: FAIL. Two proven defects in the safety gate (all measured at 5b19e6a in a fresh probe repo /tmp/reclaim-probe2, engine.reclaimable_worktrees called directly). D1 (data loss on a live lane) - src/teamwork/engine.py:4261: the only liveness signal is the top-dir mtime '(now - here.stat().st_mtime)/60 < stale_minutes', and engine.py:4262 status is 'git status --porcelain', which is blind to gitignored build output. A lane that is actively building keeps its tree 'clean' and its top dir old. Probe: worktree .pi/worktrees/live containing only ./build/obj.o (gitignored), dir aged 120m -> git status --porcelain = '' -> reclaimable_worktrees(root,60) = ['/tmp/reclaim-probe2/proj/.pi/worktrees/live'], and cmd_clean then runs 'git worktree remove --force' (engine.py:4316). A gitignored build cache inside an active lane is deleted while the lane holds it. The docstring claim ('so removing it cannot lose a commit', engine.py:4218-4221) is true for commits and false for the lane's work; nothing consults the slot lease, the task queue, or a lock file. Fix: skip worktrees referenced by any doing/selfcheck task or live slot lease, and treat ignored files as dirty (git status --porcelain --ignored or a newer-than check on any file mtime, not just the top dir). D2 (prefix test matches a sibling-shaped dir) - src/teamwork/engine.py:4258: 'here.as_posix().startswith((project / TEAM_ROOT_DIR.split("/")[0]).as_posix())' compares string prefixes without a separator. Probe: /tmp/reclaim-probe2/proj/.pi-evil/x -> startswith('/tmp/reclaim-probe2/proj/.pi') = True -> reclaimed even though it is not inside .pi/. (The <project>-evil/.pi/x variant IS correctly rejected: startswith('/…/proj/.pi') = False.) Fix: use here.resolve().is_relative_to((project/'.pi').resolve()). Note: guard discrimination is otherwise correct - clean+merged+old reclaimed; dirty NOT reclaimed; unmerged commit (merge-base --is-ancestor != 0) NOT reclaimed; younger than threshold NOT reclaimed; main checkout NOT reclaimed (also via a .pi/alias-main symlink -> resolve() catches it); /tmp/outside NOT reclaimed; --no-worktrees opts out (test_no_worktrees_leaves_them_alone passes); --force at engine.py:4316 is only reached after both checks, not a bypass. --stale-minutes defaults to 60 and is not validated for negative values (harmless, everything looks stale -> guarded only by the other checks). Fix: add a CLI-level case asserting the refusal (rc != 0, both PR ids in stderr, neither record changed status) and the MAJOR-1 merged-competitor case; then the record claim is true. MINOR-3 (message and predicate disagree): the listing filter at src/teamwork/engine.py:923-928 drops only merged records while _handle_is_ambiguous drops merged and closed, so a closed record can be named as "open" in the refusal text. Filter by the same predicate and include the status. ## T-0003 · Code scanning: py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data in scripts/privacy_scan.py  (security-auditor)
- date: 2026-10-20
- priority: P0
- owner: security-auditor role (work by: SecurityAuditor)
- reviewer: Reviewer-3

- self-check: [pass] Re-read scripts/privacy_scan.py (the _fingerprint/write_reveal_file helpers, the argparse contract, the findings loop and the report print), the 4 new tests and the two doc edits. The alert is a true positive: --reveal sent finding['match'] - the raw credential - to stdout, which in CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml:201, docs.yml:68) is a retained, world-readable log, defeating the masking that exists to stop a failing log from republishing a secret. The sink is now closed: --reveal appends only an unsalted sha256:<12 hex> fingerprint (not usable as the credential, no prefix of it), --reveal-to PATH exports raw values to a file the operator names, created O_EXCL at 0600, refused if it exists (exit 2), with a stderr WARNING and the partial file unlinked on error; match is popped before the report is built so --json cannot leak it either. Residual risk (a plaintext remediation file on disk, by operator request) is written up in docs/Team/AuditReport.md 7.1. --reveal does not degrade silently: help text, module docstring and docs/guide/cli.md all state the new contract. | test: PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py @e921a4b -> 51 passed; mutating scripts/privacy_scan.py back to HEAD (e921a4b) -> 3 failed, 48 passed incl. 'the unmasked secret must not reach stdout'; PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q @e921a4b -> 482 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check/format --check @e921a4b -> clean; mypy src @e921a4b -> clean | baseline: HEAD's scanner printed the raw ghp_... value on --reveal; no --reveal-to, no fingerprint, SCANNER_VERSION 1.2.0 | replay: PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py -k reveal

Author Security Auditor, committer CEO <ceo@agents.nidvue.com> = local git config; style fix(security): conventional.

DECISIVE (a,b): raw secret cannot reach the log. Grep of scripts/privacy_scan.py:1066-1072 pops finding["match"] before the report is built; the only output sinks are the non-JSON report loop at 1115-1119 which prints finding["evidence"] plus a optional (sha256:<12>) fingerprint, and json.dumps at 1108. Empirical, on a throwaway archive of 8980081 with a runtime-constructed synthetic token (ghp_+"A"*36) in leaky.py:

- --tree --reveal --json: raw=0, "match" keys=0, sha256: fingerprints present.
- --reveal-to PATH: rc1, created mode 600, file contains 2 raw lines location<TAB>rule<TAB>sha256:<12><TAB>value, raw token in stdout=0, PATH absent from stdout (only a stderr WARNING naming it).
- --reveal-to on an existing path: rc2 "refusing to overwrite existing file ...".

NON-VACUITY (c): in the /tmp/rv-sec copy, restored the leak (evidence=match under --reveal) -> test_reveal_never_prints_the_unmasked_secret FAILS at tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py:514 "assert planted not in out, the unmasked secret must not reach stdout", 1 failed / 1 passed. Fix restored -> green.

GATES (d), all at 8980081: PYTHONPATH=src pytest -q -> 554 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check . -> All checks passed; ruff format --check . -> 134 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success, no issues in 15 files. Tests: +4 as declared (reveal_never_prints, plain_report_omits_the_fingerprint, reveal_to_writes_a_0600_file, reveal_to_refuses_to_clobber).

TRIAGE (e): honest. AuditReport.md 7.1 names both alerts true positives (not dismissed), no false-positive language anywhere, documents why it was real (stdout is a broadcast sink, Actions logs, tee), records the fix and mutation proof, and names the residual risk explicitly (plaintext remediation file on disk by explicit request, 0600/O_EXCL/no default path/warning, CI never passes --reveal-to). Alert closure is correctly deferred to GitHub auto-close on the next CodeQL scan of the merged commit rather than claimed here.

MAJOR 2 - the "narrow literal" test does not enforce what it and the docs claim. tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py:472 test_shipped_allowlist_entries_are_narrow_and_justified asserts only `not any(ch in entry for ch in "*+?[]|()")` at :480, which rejects classes and alternation but accepts every other metacharacter: r"^/home/ping.$" passes that assertion and re.search matches "/home/pingX", so a dot-wildcard entry ships green despite the :479 comment "one exact literal, never a class or a wildcard". Security consequence, measured against the scanner: allow [re.compile("^/home/pingz$")] over "key stored at /home/pingz/.ssh/id_rsa" and "creds /home/pingz/.aws/credentials" -> no-allow ["home-path"], with-allow [] - the blessed line exempts any future real secret that merely sits under the home directory on the same line, which is broader than .github/privacy-allow.txt:24-25 "no secret, token or path inside the users home is blessed by this line". Limited to rules whose match is exactly the home prefix (a private-key marker is still caught), but the test meant to bound it does not. Also Board.md:26 "tree carries no unquoted path" is true only in the sense that the path is quoted inside allowlist comments, decision text and the test - it is still in the tree 7 times. Root cause: `elsewhere = re.compile(r"\bwho did not (?:pass|verif|review)\b", re.I)` has no \w* on `verif`, so it matches "who did not verif" only - never "verify"/"verified". Measured in a git-archive extraction of HEAD (.pi/Reviewer-1-Scratch/t43), one injection at a time appended to references/Roles.md, `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/unit/test_skill_contracts.py -q -k second_reviewer` after each: (A) "A second Reviewer must merge it once the verdict is recorded." -> 1 failed (caught); (B) "Someone who did not pass it must land the branch." -> 1 failed (caught); (C) "The Reviewer who was not the one to verify it applies the change." -> 1 passed (MISSED); variants "The Reviewer who did not verify it applies the change." / "A Reviewer who was not the verifier applies the change." / "The role that did not record the verdict integrates the change." / "An agent that merely read the diff should integrate the change." -> all 1 passed (MISSED); "Somebody besides the verifier must land the branch." -> 1 failed (caught). Regex probe: elsewhere.search("who did not verify it")=False, elsewhere.search("who did not pass it")=True. The negated-guard also cannot see a copular form ("was not the verifier"), and other_reviewer requires the modifier to precede the noun. So the round-3 defect class (a sentence naming somebody other than the verifier doing the merge) is NOT fixed - the pin passes a suite that still ships the retired two-step in a phrasing a doc rewrite can easily produce. OVER-FIRE: none - honest rows hold, full file 74 passed. GATES @6aeb001 all green, measured on a clean git-archive extraction because .pi/WT-t43 carries an uncommitted duplicate `_quoted`/ARCHIVE_QUOTE_LIMIT edit (dirty worktree; NOT on the revision): pytest -q -> 564 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check . -> All checks passed; ruff format --check . -> 135 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success, no issues in 15 files; teamwork.py links -> issues: 0; merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD -> rc=0; branch-side deletions (merge-base..HEAD) -> none (note: `git diff --diff-filter=D --name-only main HEAD` is NOT empty - it lists tests/unit/test_task_graph_integrity.py, but that is main-side: f51bbe2 added it on main after the merge-base 0fb9f1c, the merged tree contains it, and nothing is deleted by the merge). FIX: give `verif` its \w* suffix, add a copular/noun-phrase branch for "was not the verifier"/"did not verify" and "was not the one to verify", and add C + the four variants above to the test as regression cases. | PIN STILL BLIND to the verifier-negation family outside the two verbs the regex hardcodes (tests/unit/test_skill_contracts.py:1160-1165 not_the_verifier only matches not+(pass|verif|review)). At 174018e, extraction to .pi/Reviewer-3-Scratch/t43, inject one sentence into references/Roles.md, run PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/unit/test_skill_contracts.py -q -k second_reviewer -> 74 passed (MISSED) for: (b) "A reviewer that did not record the verdict integrates the change." (e2) "Once the verdict is recorded, the change is landed by the agent who did not issue it." (e5) "The branch is applied by a reviewer who did not cast the verdict." Cause: the clause has not+<a non-pass/verify/review verb>, so not_the_verifier and other_actor both fail while merging matches. Also (e1) "The Reviewer who merely read the diff lands it." MISSED: no otherness keyword at all, so any actor-anonymous second-actor phrasing is invisible. Caught correctly: (a) who was not the one to verify it; (c) whoever did not pass it; (d) Reviewer other than the verifier; (e4) Somebody besides the verifier. Over-fire all green (AGENTS.md only-role-to-pass/merge, different-role-verifies, wrapped "there is no second Reviewer step", --no-merge docs), full file 74 passed, whole suite 585 passed 2 skipped, ruff check/format/mypy clean, links 0, merge-tree rc=0, no deletions. Defect is confined to the pin: widen not_the_verifier to not+<word>+<any verdict noun/verb> (record|issue|cast|sign|write|give|return|deliver|decide|verdict|ruling|approval|blessing) and add an actor-anonymous branch (whoever|anyone|any one|the one) so (b)/(e1)/(e2)/(e5) cannot ship green again.

Gates at 248d441 otherwise green: pytest 575 passed 2 skipped; ruff check All checks passed; ruff format --check 135 files already formatted; mypy src Success 15 files; links 0 issues; privacy --tree (no --history) PASS 0. REQUIRED FIX: rebase onto main and drop the D-20261020-CEO-4 block from T-0049, leaving only the D-CEO-17 `- evidence: ` prefix (1 file, 1 line); the D-CEO-4 record ships with T-0048/P-0053 which carries its implementation and allowlist literals.

Docs: SKILL.md roster now reviewer-1+reviewer-2 with the fan-out/claim text and pulse prompt step 4, roles/reviewer.md claim protocol, references/Concurrency.md lane-concurrency rule, references/Adapters.md Pi column rewritten to the tools this install actually has (subagent fan-out, contact_supervisor, subagent_supervisor) with a verification note; I hand-checked the Adapters table renders with 6 cells per row and smoke-tested init/pulse/review --claim/--release/queue on a throwaway 2-lane team in .pi/ScratchSmoke. - reviewer evidence: Shape @650c3c4: git rev-parse HEAD -> 650c3c4b32ac9bb2625f15fee1700bf6e5902d0c; git diff --diff-filter=D --name-only $(git merge-base main HEAD) HEAD -> empty; git merge-tree --write-tree main HEAD -> rc=0. grep -n _RE_JSON_SECRET src/teamwork/engine.py -> 0 hits (bare-regex arm gone). ROUND-3 DEFECT CLOSED, safe extraction git archive HEAD | tar -x, PYTHONPATH=src .venv/bin/python: 8 JSON placeholder lines ({"api_key": "${API_KEY}"}, {"apiKey": "replace_me"}, {"token": "changeme"}, {"api_key": "process.env.KEY"}, <your-api-key>, YOUR_API_KEY, xxxxxxxx, ...) -> gate findings=0 AND mask_secret_shapes byte-identical for all 8. Real runtime-built JSON credential -> gate rule=json-secret, mask -> {"api_key": "<redacted-json-secret>"}, value absent. All 5 engine.CREDENTIAL_RULES probes (private-key, api-token, env-secret, json-secret, high-entropy-secret) -> reported, masked with <redacted-RULE>, re-scan residue=0. Prose non-regression byte-identical: sk-learn-pipeline, the sk-timeout-wrapper helper, GITHUB_TOKEN=abcdefgh, 40-char sha, URL, normal sentence. Record: D-20261020-CEO-4 still states the measured exemption scope limit (one finding VALUE, not one file/variable) and pins no finding/line/commit count. Gates @650c3c4: pytest -q -> 593 passed 2 skipped; ruff check -> All checks passed; ruff format --check -> 136 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success 15 files; teamwork.py links -> 0 issues; privacy_scan --tree --history --allow -> PASS 0 findings. MUTANT mask_secret_shapes returns input -> tests/unit/test_context_economy.py 4 failed 30 passed (incl. test_the_archive_mask_follows_the_gate_for_json_config_shapes); restored -> 34 passed.
- findings history: FAIL @aa43e2f (base main c0b69ae). The masking half (A) is real but INCOMPLETE, and the decision record pins two numbers that do not reproduce. All probes below run from `git archive aa43e2f | tar -x` at .pi/Reviewer-2-Scratch/t48 with PYTHONPATH=src and the repo venv; scratch removed after. Shape and gates are green and are not charged. BLOCKER 1 - `mask_secret_shapes` covers only 2 of the scanner's 4 credential rules, so the headline claim is false. src/teamwork/engine.py:6467-6478 masks only `_RE_BEARER` (engine.py:6459) and `_RE_PASSWORD` (engine.py:6460). It does not mask `private-key` (scripts/privacy_scan.py:210), `json-secret` or `high-entropy-secret`, which are armed on the same archive line. Measured by calling `archive_append` on a temp root with evidence "the private-key rule fires on <PEM RSA private-key header>": the raw header survives verbatim into docs/Team/Archive.md and `ps.scan_lines(<archive>, "docs/Team/Archive.md", "tree", ps.RULES, allow)` then reports `['private-key']` - i.e. exactly the failure T-0048 exists to prevent, on a different rule. Same for a bare 32-char base64 high-entropy value (masked text still reports `high-entropy-secret`) and for '{"apiKey": "<32-char value>"}' (still reports `json-secret`). This directly falsifies docs/Team/Decisions.md:438 "so no new fabricated or real value can enter the archive" and :440 "shows a credential in a verdict never reaches Archive.md" - tests/unit/test_context_economy.py:900 only ever probes the two masked shapes, so the suite cannot see the gap. FIX: mask every credential rule the gate arms (at minimum private-key headers, JSON secret values and bare high-entropy runs), or narrow the claim to the two shapes actually masked and add the missing shapes as tasks. BLOCKER 2 - the archive entry's own header line is never masked, and a token there lands in the archive raw. src/teamwork/engine.py:6506 writes `f"## {t['id']} · {t['title']} ({owner})"` with no `_quoted`/`mask_secret_shapes`; `QUOTED_EVIDENCE_FIELDS` at engine.py:6492 lists only the four evidence fields and is never referenced anywhere (`grep -rn QUOTED_EVIDENCE_FIELDS src/` -> the definition only, dead constant). Measured: `archive_append` with title "leak <token>" -> the raw token is in Archive.md and the line reports `api-token` with the shipped allowlist. Evidence line 6506 and the dead constant at 6492. BLOCKER 3 - docs/Team/Decisions.md:440 pins a credential-hit count that does not reproduce. Text: "the same command without `--allow` -> the three findings are the only credential-rule hits, all three inside a quoted archive evidence line". Measured at aa43e2f, `privacy_scan.py --tree --history` (no `--allow`) -> 7 credential-rule findings over 4 distinct lines: docs/Team/Archive.md:744 (api-token) and :745 (env-secret x2), plus .github/privacy-allow.txt:43 (api-token) and tests/unit/test_privacy_scan.py:507 (api-token) - so 4 of 7 are NOT inside the archive; they are this PR's own new allowlist entry and its mirrored expected-list literal in the test. The tree-only run is FAIL 8 (2 critical at allow.txt:43 and test:507). Falsifier met for the same class that failed T-0034 rounds 1-8: the commit that documents a count changes it. - date: 2026-10-20
- priority: P0
- owner: ceo role (work by: CEO)
- reviewer: Reviewer-2
- task / issue: -
- area: -
- self-check: [pass] re-read the five diffs against the CI failure list; each fix addresses the reported assertion on its own line | test: pytest -q @3a83e27 -> 598 passed, 1 skipped; the 5 CI-failing files -> 102 passed; ruff/format/mypy clean || [pass] re-read the round-1 blocker against the code; verified the exclusion key and both artifact-path writes are posix and that no str(relative_to) remains on a published path | test: pytest -q @bb5b25d -> 598 passed, 1 skipped; test_command_surface.py -> 9 passed
- author evidence: 
- reviewer evidence: bb5b25d: diff --stat 6f96366..bb5b25d -> 5 files +37/-10; merge-tree --write-tree main bb5b25d -> rc=0; engine.py:2823/2834/2870 all .as_posix() (3/3 published paths); grep "str(.*relative_to" -> only engine.py:6654 archive_append (display-only, no manifest consumer); pytest tests/integration/test_command_surface.py -> 9 passed, test_ledger_export_does_not_pin_the_append_only_archive asserts "docs/Team/Archive.md" not in paths and Project/Decisions present; skill_sync non-vacuous: deleted src/one.txt -> rels!=expected, missing=["src/one.txt"]; tiers recomputed slim=226449->226 kB, standard=336479->336 kB, full=586267->586 kB (16 inst) match SKILL.md; privacy_scan.py:622-631 POSIX else-branch asserts exactly 0o600, nt-branch in (0o600,0o666) with exclusivity pinned by test_reveal_to_refuses_to_clobber_an_existing_file (code==2, content preserved); test_worktree_reclaim._age follow_symlinks fallback is test-helper only, engine._worktree_recently_written:4617 still uses child.stat() following; sweep: S_IMODE only test_privacy_scan.py:622, "== 0o600" only :631 under else, integrity_fixes.py:543 test is skipif(nt) (untouched skip, not weakened by this diff); stat().st_size hits are manifest bytes/display/LFS-threshold, no path; gates: pytest -q -> 598 passed, 1 skipped; ruff check . -> All checks passed; ruff format --check . -> 137 files already formatted; mypy src -> Success: no issues found in 15 source files; links -> issues: 0; git log 6f96366..bb5b25d -> CEO <ceo@agents.nidvue.com> author+committer on both 3a83e27 and bb5b25d
- findings history: BLOCKER engine.py:2870: the posix conversion was applied to the published artifact paths (2821,2832) but NOT to the exclusion key archive_rel = str(archive_path(root).relative_to(project)). On Windows that yields docs\Team\Archive.md while a["path"] is now docs/Team/Archive.md, so the equality in the external filter at 2871 is never true and Archive.md is published as an artifact. Proof (PureWindowsPath simulation of both sides): archive_rel = "docs\\Team\\Archive.md", artifacts = [Project.md, Plan.md, Architecture.md, Decisions.md, Archive.md]; on posix artifacts = the same list minus Archive.md. That re-breaks the deliberate guarantee "Archive.md is append-only and must not be pinned" and makes tests/integration/test_command_surface.py::test_ledger_export_does_not_pin_the_append_only_archive fail on windows-latest (it passes on Linux, so CI stays red on Windows with a different test). Fix: archive_rel = archive_path(root).relative_to(project).as_posix(). Non-blocking: test_privacy_scan.py:629 accepts 0o666 on nt - legitimate (Windows has no POSIX mode bits, os.chmod only toggles FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY and a fresh file reads back 0o666) and exclusivity is genuinely pinned by test_reveal_to_refuses_to_clobber_an_existing_file (code==2 + content preserved), but 0o600 can never occur there so assert only 0o666 with that comment. Non-blocking: engine.py:6654 str(path.relative_to(project_of(root))) is the same non-portable class, latent/display-only.
- branch / merge: ceo/t-0051-windows-ci-is-red-five-portability-defec / bf82eaa

Evidence: `python scripts/teamwork.py loop; echo rc=$?` @a1a66e6 -> "> CONTINUE / ! 1 task(s) not finished / ! 1 deliverable(s) not review-passed / ! 1 branch(es) not merged into main" rc=1; `python scripts/teamwork.py audit; echo rc=$?` @a1a66e6 -> "# problems found: 4" rc=1. So the branch replaces the old red-state failure with the inverted one: the tree is red for its own guard either way. Mechanism (why the flip cannot be true at its own revision): `loop` counts T-0056 itself as an unfinished, review-pending, unmerged branch, so AC-11 is self-referential - it can only be green AFTER the merge that this row is part of, never on the commit that declares it. Remedy, any of: (a) keep `red (G-4)` and make G-4 name the self-reference (loop counts this task) instead of claiming closure; (b) state the row as conditional with its falsifier, e.g. `green once merged (T-0056)` plus a guard exemption/EXEMPT entry for AC-11 that records why, so the guard does not assert a state the tree cannot have; (c) drop the two proof commands from the AC-11 cell to clause-level checks that are true pre-merge (e.g. `audit` on a tree with no open review task) - but then the row must name them. Non-vacuity of the guard is fine: it caught this. Also note the gap row G-4 claims "loop prints STOP and audit finds zero problems" as present fact, which the same two runs falsify.

(6) and the pin --branch 9.9.9 (7) -> "R9 DRIFT README.md:7" only, exit 2; so a rollback sentence ending in a period and both compare forms are now caught by no rule, which is the class of gap this item was meant to close. 3) R2 `--points-at` - the four mandated cases PASS on my own fixture (linear v1.0.0/v1.1.0/v1.1.2/v1.1.3/v1.1.4 + filler + 1.1.5): detach at v1.1.4 -> R1 ok, R2 ok "newer than 4 tag(s)"; main at 1.1.5 -> ok; detach at v1.0.0 -> "R2 DRIFT ... v1.1.0; v1.1.2; v1.1.3; v1.1.4" (not its own tag); untagged 1.1.3 commit between -> "R2 DRIFT v1.1.3; v1.1.4"; filler after v1.1.3 -> DRIFT v1.1.4 only. But three new defects: (a) the ok detail arithmetic is wrong for non-version tags at HEAD - 3 tags at HEAD (v1.1.0 + docs-a + docs-b) -> "R2 ok HEAD 1.1.0 is newer than -2 tag(s)" while Architecture.md:376 promises "N-1"; `len(tags) - len(own_tags)` subtracts every tag at HEAD but counts only version tags. (b) the rule is main-line-only but the contract does not say so: a maintenance branch off v1.0.0 with v1.0.1 tagged, checked out at v1.0.1 -> "R2 DRIFT ... v1.1.0; v1.1.2", so a patch release from a maintenance line can never be green; either the linear-main assumption is stated or the rule must compare against the newest tag on HEAD ancestry (`git describe --tags --abbrev=0`). (c) the gate is vacuous on the very build it exists for, because .github/workflows/release.yml:33-36 actions/checkout has no fetch-depth (default 1): `git clone --depth 1 --branch v1.1.2` -> tags=[v1.1.2] -> "R1 ok 1 tag(s)" + "R2 ok HEAD 1.1.2 is newer than 0 tag(s)", i.e. green while evaluating one tag. The contract must require the full tag set (fetch-depth: 0 / `git fetch --tags --force`) and say so, since with --points-at an incomplete tag list silently passes. 4) Fourth contract defect still open in the same section (pre-existing, not introduced here): Architecture.md:328-329 says "a non-200 response ... is the failed API call of exit 2" while :479-482 makes PyPI 404 DRIFT/ok; the implementation only reconciles this because HttpError carries .status (R8 404 -> ok or DRIFT, R8 500/503 -> fatal SKIP exit 2, R4/R5 404 -> fatal). State the 404 exception in 11.1.4 or drop it from 11.2.4. 5) Regression @986fecb: `pytest -q tests/unit/test_portability.py` -> 25 passed; `teamwork links` -> "# issues: 0"; `diff <(git show 8db0b97:docs/Team/Architecture.md | sed -n "/^### 11.3/,/$p") <(... 986fecb ...)` -> empty, §11.3.1/§11.4/§11.5 and all six digest literals (a01d9f0cbee641c3, ebfc2538e3d3d605, 726bc547afc41ead, acec49f6cd1fe99a, cd43f09293a49c68, 545fc160...) count-identical, illustrative markers 4 -> 4; scope `git diff 8db0b97..986fecb --name-only` -> docs/Team/Architecture.md only; `git merge-tree --write-tree main 986fecb` -> rc=0. 6) Coordination hazard for the merge: `git grep points-at` across every local branch -> no match; developer-1 P-0073 still carries the v-only `TAGGED_TOKEN` and builds the tag as `f"v{match.group(1)}"`, so the only existing implementation becomes non-conforming on R2 and R9 the moment this lands while CI runs it (releaseengineer T-0003). - findings history: FAIL @e4cd4c1 (Reviewer-3). The digest machinery is CORRECT and independently reproduced; the defects are in exit-code precedence and three rule implementations. BLOCKER 1 - R3 downgrades an unreadable npm input to DRIFT (exit 1) instead of input error (exit 2), violating 11.1.1/11.1.4/11.3.1. Proof, driving main() with only the two network seams replaced: c._http_get_bytes raising c.HttpError(url,503) -> "R3 DRIFT 1.1.0: tarball unreadable: https://registry.npmjs.org/@nidvue/teamwork/-/teamwork-1.1.0.tgz: HTTP 503" and main() returned 2 only because R4/R5 also failed; with R4/R5 healthy, _emit([...R3 fatal=False...]) returned 1. Same for a tarball with none of the three payload prefixes: rule_r3_npm_payloads -> status DRIFT fatal False, findings ("1.1.0: tarball unreadable: npm tarball: none of the shipped payload paths are present",), _emit -> 1, though 11.3.1 says "an input error (exit 2), not an empty payload". Cause: scripts/check_release_consistency.py:767 and :776 catch InputError inside the R3 loop and append a finding instead of setting fatal=True. Effect: a network flake or a malformed published tarball is indistinguishable from real release drift, so a CI gate cannot route infra from drift. BLOCKER 2 - release-exceptions.json evidence cites a digest form the contract forbids. e4cd4c1:release-exceptions.json says "npm 1.1.1 payload digest 0786a4ff3c2f84ba"; 11.3.1 (docs/Team/Architecture.md) says path-sorted-with-generator gives 0786a4ff3c2f84ba and "those are not 11.3 digests and must never be published as such". Measured on the real tarball: payload-path-sorted-including-scripts/build_pi_package.py = 0786a4ff3c2f84ba; payload-line-sorted-excluding-it = 726bc547afc41ead. The checker itself computes 726bc547afc41ead (and its docstring says so), so the committed record contradicts both the contract and the code it documents. Replace with 726bc547afc41ead (verified: no commit in 402 scanned reproduces it). MAJOR 3 - R8 mis-parses the uvx form, flagging correct prose. _channel_claims() takes the token after "uvx --from pkg" as a console script even when it is an ordinary word: "`uvx --from teamwork-agents` is the fastest way to try it." -> [("teamwork-agents","is")]; "There is no `uvx --from teamwork-agents` channel yet." -> [("teamwork-agents","channel")]. Appended to docs/index.md at e4cd4c1 this yields "docs/index.md:95: channel runs to, which is not a [project.scripts] entry" and ":97: channel runs channel, ..." (false DRIFT). _negated_install() also never matches a uvx/pipx negation, so a line that denies the channel is still a claim. MAJOR 4 - R6 false negative: any line matching FOREIGN_VERSION is skipped whole, so a real project version on the same line disappears. Appending "Install Teamwork 1.0.2 to match python: 3.12.13 in this guide." to docs/index.md at e4cd4c1 -> NOT FLAGGED (line 95), while the sibling line "Install with npm install teamwork-agents at version 1.0.2 today." is flagged. The skip must be per token, not per line. MINOR 5 - R4 compares GitHub release names against _version_tags(), which keeps only ^vX.Y.Z$, so a legitimate non-semver release tag is reported with a self-contradicting message: with v1.1.3-rc1 created and a release {"tag_name":"v1.1.3-rc1"}, R4 -> DRIFT "release v1.1.3-rc1 has no tag v1.1.3-rc1 in git tag -l" although git tag -l lists it. MINOR 6 - tar extraction guard is name-only (scripts/check_release_consistency.py:326-331) and relies on extractall defaulting to the safe filter. That default arrives in Python 3.14 (PEP 706); the CI matrix tests 3.9, so on 3.9-3.13 a symlink member with linkname ../../.. passes the guard. Measured here: 3.14.8 raises LinkOutsideDestinationError, i.e. the guard is not what stops it. Add member.issym()/islnk() target validation. VERIFIED GOOD (do not redo): tarball 1.1.0 -> ebfc2538e3d3d605 == independent sha256sum pipeline of tag v1.1.0; tarball 1.1.2 -> a01d9f0cbee641c3 == payload of 3f4981d; both reproduced twice, once through payload_digest() and once with a hand-built find/sha256sum/sort pipeline. --offline reaches ok for none of R3/R4/R5/R8 (all SKIP with a reason); R3 exception honesty holds (adding 1.0.0 to a copy -> "exception names a traceable version, tag v1.0.0 exists; remove the entry", exit 1); missing release-exceptions.json -> exit 2; --repo-root /tmp -> 9 SKIP, exit 2; --offline --online -> one stderr line, exit 2; drift+fatal -> 2; JSON has exactly problems/rules/exit_code; stdlib only; ast.parse feature_version=(3,9) ok; not a git work tree at the scratch extraction still exits 2 (no silent pass). Implementation is correct where the pre-8db0b97 contract text was wrong: the TARBALL_LAYOUT re-layout it implements is what makes the published digests reachable at all. | FAIL @1f756a2 (Reviewer-3). All six prior findings independently RE-VERIFIED FIXED by re-running my own reproducers (exit-code precedence 503->2/404->1/timeout->2/mismatch->1/mixed->2 with drift kept in --json; digest 726bc547afc41ead reproduced from a real npm pack of @nidvue/teamwork@1.1.1 and 0786a4ff3c2f84ba gone; uvx/pipx claim now (teamwork-agents,None); R6 1.0.2 DRIFT while python: 3.12.13 is not; R4 v1.1.3-rc1 not flagged; tar sym/hardlink/absolute all InputError). Offline and online output byte-identical to e4cd4c1, same 9/26 problems, R3/R4/R5/R8 SKIP never ok, --repo-root /tmp -> 2, --offline --online -> 2, JSON keys unchanged, stdlib-only, ast.parse(3,9) clean, ruff clean. (A) BLOCKER - contract 11.3 says "Symlinks are resolved" (8db0b97 docs/Team/Architecture.md:487 and :532) but scripts/check_release_consistency.py:384 rejects EVERY link member of both the git archive (:442,:450) and the npm tarball (:475). ## T-0010 · M9-O: the R8 rule must not flag a risk row that warns against a wrong install channel Measured on commit e4cd4c1: the offline R8 scan reports 'DRIFT docs/Team/Risks.md:17: channel names teamwork, the project ships teamwork-agents'. That row is risk R-8, whose whole point is that the name teamwork on PyPI belongs to another project, so the line names the channel in order to warn against it - it is not an advertisement. R8 exists to catch an advertised channel that would not resolve, not a sentence that quotes a bad channel as a hazard. As written, the checker makes 'make ci' fail on correct prose and the only escape is to delete the warning or misuse a marker meant for another meaning. Decide the narrowest correct fix and implement it with a test: either extend R8's existing negation handling (it already ignores 'there is no pip install and no venv') to a channel line inside a warning/negation context, or scope the channel-line match to lines that present the command as advice. Do not weaken the rule for a genuine advertisement: a test must still prove that an unmarked 'pip install teamwork-agents' line on a non-PyPI channel is DRIFT. Note: R8's tokenizer is being reworked in the same file (task T-0005 round 3), so coordinate on the file - work on branch developer-1/t-0005 is the owner's; if you need a separate branch, state the base sha you used and keep the edit minimal and conflict-free. Evidence: .venv/bin/python scripts/check_release_consistency.py --offline on e4cd4c1.  (developer)

(1) release-consistency is blocking: no continue-on-error, same runs-on ubuntu-latest, same checkout@3d3c42e... # v7 + setup-python@5fda3b9... # v7 pins as every other job, same cache-less setup-python style as privacy (correct: the gate installs nothing). (2) The decisive design point: the old on:push paths-ignore would have skipped the workflow entirely for a docs-only push, so the new job would be skipped exactly when docs drift - the gate would be worthless. paths-ignore is therefore removed and replaced by a scope job computing the changed set; the prose/media set is byte-equivalent to docs.yml's paths: list, and the other jobs gate on needs.scope.outputs.code. (3) Each path filter entry is justified in the commit body from the rules that read it: docs/**, README.md (R6/R9), CHANGELOG.md (R7), AGENTS.md (R8); the rest stay in the set because docs.yml triggers on them and the lists must not diverge. (4) --offline only: R3/R4/R5/R8 need npm/GitHub/PyPI credentials and rate-limit headroom and an unauthenticated 403 would make the gate flaky; the online half belongs to release.yml. (5) Pin convention: every uses is a 40-char sha with a # vN comment, byte-identical to the pre-existing jobs - no floating major tag added, so no new annotation. (6) Verified no job lost: before 7 jobs, after 9, removed=NONE, added=scope,release-consistency. | test: python -c 'yaml.safe_load(open(".github/workflows/ci.yml"))' (PyYAML 6.0.3) -> 9 jobs ['build','committer-guard','docs','lint','privacy','release-consistency','scope','test','typecheck'], removed=NONE; bash -n on all 17 run blocks -> rc=0 every one; scope classifier replay over 10 event shapes (root commit, docs-only push, code push, PR, empty commit, mixed, unresolvable before, workflow_dispatch, tag push, docs-only PR-branch push) -> code=true/false as designed in all 10; python scripts/check_release_consistency.py --offline in this worktree -> exit=2 (checker not merged here); in a clone carrying developer-1's scripts/check_release_consistency.py + release-exceptions.json with real history and tags -> exit=1 on R6/R9 drift; on a synthetic consistent tree -> exit=0; with CHANGELOG.md deleted -> exit=2; ruff check . -> All checks passed!; ruff format --check . -> 139 files already formatted || [pass] Re-read both diffs against 3340f46: the ci.yml scope fallback (merge base with the default branch, else the root commit, undetermined -> run), the release-consistency job (S = A - O applied to the checker's R2 findings, exit 2 preserved, other findings still fail) and docs/Team/Release.md (no paths-ignore claimed anywhere, the prose/media set unchanged and still identical to docs.yml's paths list, the scope job described, the offline/online split stated). - findings history: BLOCKER 1 - adding `push: tags: ["v*"]` makes the new job exit 1 on every tag push, by construction. ci.yml:33 adds tags to the push trigger and ci.yml:321-351 release-consistency has no `needs:`/`if:`, so the job runs on a tag push and checks out the tagged commit (HEAD == the tag). R2 then requires HEAD version STRICTLY greater than every tag, which R1 already forbids (R1 requires version.py AT the tag == the tag). Measured: fresh clone of this repo + `git tag v1.1.2` at main (version.py = 1.1.2), `git checkout v1.1.2`, `python scripts/check_release_consistency.py --offline` -> exit=1, "R2 DRIFT HEAD version 1.1.2 is not greater than tag v1.1.2", "# release consistency: 9 problem(s)". Same at v1.1.0/v1.0.2/v1.0.0 (exit=1 each). So any state where R1 passes guarantees R2 fails at its own tag: the commit comment at ci.yml:30-32 ("the gate must also see the tag that was just created") describes exactly the condition that reddens it. This blocks M9-G (tag v1.1.2) and every future release tag. Fix needs a joint decision: drop the tag trigger, or make R2 exempt the case HEAD is exactly the tag under test, or run the gate on the preceding branch push only. Contract owner is Architect (Architecture.md has no section 11 on main yet, so the frozen text cannot arbitrate). BLOCKER 2 - the revision contradicts the frozen contract it cites. ci.yml:3 says "Contract: docs/Team/Architecture.md (section 9)". Architecture.md:216-217 (on main) still says "On `push` the prose and media paths are `paths-ignore`d", and Release.md:60 says "`ci.yml` therefore declares `paths-ignore` for the prose/media set". Both are now false: `grep -n paths-ignore .github/workflows/ci.yml` on 3340f46 -> no match. The deliverable changes the trigger policy without amending the two documents that define it, and this class of prose-vs-workflow drift is exactly what R6/R8/R9 do not detect. Either Architect amends section 9 (ADR + decision) or the trigger change is reverted. docs.yml:5-6 is stale in the same way ("`ci.yml` ignores documentation-only paths"). MAJOR 3 - the online half is never run by anything. The job comment at ci.yml:335-336 says "The online half belongs in the release workflow or a manual run", but `grep -n check_release_consistency .github/workflows/release.yml` -> NONE FOUND. R3/R4/R5 (npm registry, GitHub Releases) are SKIP in every automated run, i.e. npm-tarball/tag/Release agreement - the original M9 motivation - is unenforced in CI. Task text permits omitting it, so not the fail reason, but it needs a follow-up task, not a comment claiming a home that does not exist. MAJOR 4 - acceptance criterion "It must ... be listed in the required checks" is unmet and unaddressed. `gh api repos/Nidvue/Teamwork/branches/main/protection` -> strict=true, 15 contexts, none matching "release consistency (offline - docs + tags + version)". No follow-up task records who will add it. MINOR 5 - scope step fail-open on a multi-commit push when `before` cannot be resolved. ci.yml:99-105 falls back to `git diff HEAD^ HEAD` (or `diff-tree --root`), which only inspects the LAST commit. Measured in a sandbox with commit1=src change, commit2=docs-only, BEFORE_SHA=40 zeros (new branch) or an unresolvable sha (post-rewrite): code=false, so the 3x3 matrix and every other gated job skip although code changed in the range. Correct results require the full range; the fallback silently narrows it. (With a valid `before` the range is right: code=true.) MINOR 6 - commit body and self_note both claim "bash -n on all 17 run blocks"; 3340f46 actually has 19 run blocks (17 before, +2 for scope/release-consistency; `yaml.safe_load` + count -> 19, bash -n rc=0 on all 19). Correct for 3340f46^, wrong for the commit it describes. VERIFIED GOOD (all executed): YAML valid; old jobs [build, committer-guard, docs, lint, privacy, test, typecheck], new jobs + [release-consistency, scope], REMOVED=[]; release-consistency has no needs/if/continue-on-error and its run step is the bare `python scripts/check_release_consistency.py --offline` (no `|| true`, no `; exit 0`, no prefix); gate exit codes measured absent-script=2 and real-drift=1; `paths-ignore` gone from on.push; scope job exists; classifier verified on 16 real event shapes (docs-only/docs-Team-only/CHANGELOG-only/AGENTS-only/README-only/assets/mkdocs -> false; version.py/src/scripts -> true; mixed docs+src -> true; tag push -> false; PR + workflow_dispatch -> true); the ci.yml PROSE regex and docs.yml paths: list agree 11/11 with no member missing on either side; no deadlock from needs:scope because on pull_request the `if` short-circuits to true and every required context is reported (skips only happen on push, which gates no merge); `teamwork links` -> 0 issues; the commit touches only .github/workflows/ci.yml; 3340f46 and 1c3989e have identical trees and the same parent 8e709a0 (amend claim confirmed). | BLOCKER 1 (.github/workflows/ci.yml:120-135, scope fallback): the scope job still UNDER-REPORTS and silently skips the 3x3 matrix on shipping-code pushes. The fallback merges BASE with "origin/${DEFAULT_BRANCH}", but after a push origin/main already points at the pushed HEAD, so merge-base(HEAD,origin/main)==HEAD, and the branch "elif [ "${BASE}" = "${HEAD_REV}" ]" (ci.yml:132-135, documented as "HEAD is the root commit") is taken, which runs git diff-tree --root -r HEAD and lists ONLY the LAST commit. MEASURED (fresh repo: c1 root src/a.py+docs/d.md, c2 SRC change to src/a.py, c3 docs-only; bare remote; fresh CI clone, origin/main==github.sha): merge-base(HEAD,origin/main)=HEAD (YES), TRUE push range (root..HEAD)="docs/d.md src/a.py", scope output "scope: changed -> docs/d.md" / "scope: code=false" => the matrix is skipped although src/a.py changed. Same under-report on a genuine rewrite (force-push dropping the SRC commit, before=OLD unresolvable, github.sha=NEW) => code=false while the pushed range changed src/a.py. So the claimed MINOR 5 fix is NOT in effect. FIX: enter the diff-tree --root branch only when HEAD truly has no parents (git rev-list --parents -n1 HEAD has one field); otherwise set UNDETERMINED=1 so code=true. BLOCKER 2 (.github/workflows/ci.yml:357-470 + branch protection): the job is now a REQUIRED status check but its checker is not in the tree. git cat-file -e 34c5c34:scripts/check_release_consistency.py -> ABSENT (also absent on main). MEASURED gate run on the branch head: "python: cannot open file .../scripts/check_release_consistency.py: [Errno 2] No such file or directory", then "::error::the checker produced no report: Expecting value: line 1 column 1 (char 0)", GATE EXIT=2. gh api repos/Nidvue/Teamwork/branches/main/protection now lists 16 contexts including "release consistency (offline - docs + tags + version)" (strict=true) - so main is blocked on a check that cannot run until developer task T-0005 lands. MEASURED additionally: with a checker present, origin/main tip is RED today (R6 x7, R8, R9, 9-10 problems) - the prose drift must be fixed in the same landing. FIX: land T-0005 (checker) and the R6/R8/R9 prose fixes first, or the PR must not be merged on its own; do not add a "checker missing => skip" branch. MAJOR 3 (.github/workflows/ci.yml:375-386,425-460): the workflow re-implements frozen rule semantics (S = A - O) in a python heredoc - a second source of truth, and it parses the checker English text with re.findall(r"\btag (v\d+\.\d+\.\d+)\b", detail). MEASURED: with the compliant checker (d998801, comparable = reachable - at_head) the exemption is a no-op on a tag push (R2 ok, no R2 finding) and the stale main still fails (R2 DRIFT, 10 problems, GATE EXIT=1) - so it is behaviourally correct today, but a checker that words the message differently makes the exemption inert, and a finding naming several tags is exempted all-or-nothing. FIX: say the online half lands with T-0007. (c) the job fails the workflow - no continue-on-error, no || true, no set +e, GATE_RC captured and propagated (exit 1 drift, 2 unreadable) - MEASURED GATE EXIT=1 and EXIT=2; (d) the R2 exemption does not hide a genuine drift - MEASURED stale main: R2 DRIFT NOT exempted, 9 problems, GATE EXIT=1, and with the compliant checker 10 problems, GATE EXIT=1; MEASURED tag push at v1.1.2: the naive R2 finding is exempted and the tag is not red on the tag it publishes; (e) the prose path lists are IDENTICAL - ci.yml PROSE (ci.yml:100) vs docs.yml paths: compared over a 23-path universe with GitHub glob semantics, zero mismatches; ## T-0007 · M9-N: release.yml must fetch the full tag set (fetch-depth: 0) Measured twice independently. .github/workflows/release.yml:33-36 checkout has no fetch-depth, so on a release build a shallow clone makes R1/R2 vacuous. Reviewer-1: 'git clone --depth 1 --branch v1.1.2 -> R1 ok 1 tag(s) + R2 ok ... 0 tag(s)'. Reviewer-4 found the same from the CI side and that the online half has no home at all - grep -n check_release_consistency .github/workflows/release.yml -> NONE FOUND, so R3/R4/R5 are SKIP in every automated run. Required: (1) add fetch-depth: 0 to the release.yml checkout so the gate sees every tag; (2) run the checker --online in release.yml (the job already holds a release token) so R3/R4/R5 stop being permanently SKIP, or record a measured decision why not; (3) prove the gate's exit code propagates - non-zero must fail the release job, not be swallowed; (4) fix the ci.yml:3 header citing Architecture.md 9 for path filtering (Architecture.md:216-217 and Release.md:60 still claim paths-ignore exists). Deps: architect T-0003 freezes the fetch-depth contract text first.  (release-engineer)
- date: 2026-10-21
- priority: P0
- owner: release-engineer role (work by: ReleaseEngineer)
- reviewer: Reviewer-2
- task / issue: -
- area: release
- self-check: [pass] Re-read the committed release.yml: the header contract line now names section 11; the consistency job is first in jobs: and build needs it, attest needs build, release needs [build, attest], so the gate precedes the publish in the dependency graph and not only in the step order. Checked every run: block with bash -n and compiled both python heredocs. Re-read Architecture.md 11.2.2 (5a301c8) for the O exclusion and the fetch-depth: 0 rule, and 11.1.1 for the exit codes. Confirmed the file has no continue-on-error and no || true outside the grep -c guard. Confirmed git diff --stat touches only .github/workflows/release.yml; the checker copy and release-exceptions.json stay untracked. | test: python3 -c "import yaml;d=yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/release.yml'));print(sorted(d['jobs']))" -> ['attest', 'build', 'consistency', 'release']; bash -n on all 8 run blocks + compile of both python heredocs -> 0 failures; gate body extracted from the YAML run against three fixtures -> exit 2 (checker absent) / exit 1 (R7 drift) / exit 0 (consistent, 0 problems); git diff --stat -> 1 file changed, .github/workflows/release.yml only || [pass] Re-read the whole diff of .github/workflows/release.yml at 7cbffee: the R2 exemption and its false comment are gone, the R5 exemption is narrow (exact rule + exact detail string + reason), the pre-publish gate retries only exit 2 and never a drift, the post-publish step fails on any non-zero checker exit and polls a briefly invisible Release, the raw exit code is mapped to 0/1/2, and the dead 'tag {tag} has no GitHub Release' branch is removed. No secret is written to the tree; the token is github.token in the runner env only. | test: python3 -c "import yaml; d=yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/release.yml')); print(sorted(d['jobs']))" @7cbffee -> ['attest','build','consistency','release']; bash -n on 8 run blocks @7cbffee -> rc=0 for all; scratch-clone gate @7cbffee -> absent 2, drift 1, clean 0, unreadable 2, R5-published-tag 0, R5-other-tag 1; post-publish @7cbffee -> invisible-then-visible 0 (3 attempts), missing 1, unreadable 2 || [pass] Re-read the whole .github/workflows/release.yml against the five findings: the branch history (3 commits, all Release Engineer) carries only .github/workflows/release.yml, so neither the ci.yml carry (BLOCKER 1/2) nor the pre-fix ci.yml revision survives; the header now names only properties of this file - gate in the consistency job before the build and again after the publish, fetch-depth: 0 for the complete tag set, fails closed with exit 2 on a missing checker - and no section number or other lane's pending state (MAJOR 3/MINOR 5); the post-publish step now maps 0->0, 1->1, 2->2 and any unexpected exit ->2 with the reason in a comment, matching the pre-publish gate (MINOR 4). The untracked checker files were left untracked and are byte-identical to main's copies (md5 94bc98bd...); they were moved aside only to let the rebase check out main's tracked copies. | test: git diff --name-only $(git merge-base main HEAD)..HEAD @29f2de2 -> .github/workflows/release.yml (only); bash -n on all 8 run: blocks @29f2de2 -> 8/8 rc=0; python3 scripts/check_release_consistency.py --offline @29f2de2 -> 7 problem(s), exit 1 (R2/R6/R9 drift, R3/R4/R5/R8 skip offline) - red on the unmerged doc branches, not fixed here; python3 -c yaml jobs @29f2de2 -> ['attest','build','consistency','release']; teamwork links -> 0 issues; ruff check . && ruff format --check . -> All checks passed / 143 files already formatted
- author evidence: commit 7bcd30e; commit 7cbffee; commit e22aa55
- reviewer evidence: git diff --name-only 5f75f65..29f2de2 -> .github/workflows/release.yml only; git merge-tree --write-tree main 29f2de2 -> rc=0, tree 29a325c, main->tree diff = .github/workflows/release.yml only (ci.yml untouched); yaml jobs -> [attest, build, consistency, release]; 8 run blocks, bash -n all rc=0, 3 embedded python heredocs compile; fetch-depth: 0 on all 3 checkouts (release.yml:68,:242,:350); build needs [consistency] (release.yml:235), release needs [build, attest] (:325); no continue-on-error, only || true at release.yml:85 (grep -c, empty -> 0 -> exit 2); exit mapping 0/1/2 -> 0/1/2, other -> 2; R5 exemption exact-equality on detail 'tag <tag> has no GitHub Release' (release.yml:205-208); R2 exemption gone, grep -n 'tag (v' .github/workflows/release.yml -> empty; post-publish re-check maps exit 2 -> exit 2, ATTEMPTS=4 x 20s, publish steps precede the second checkout; git log --format='%an <%ae>' main..29f2de2 -> 3x Release Engineer <releaseengineer@agents.nidvue.com>
- findings history: FAIL @7bcd30e (Reviewer-4). 4 MAJOR + 3 MINOR. The 3 structural requirements of the task are met and measured: fetch-depth: 0 on all three checkouts (release.yml:55, :195, :303), the consistency job precedes the publish in the dependency graph (release.yml:188 build needs [consistency], :256 attest needs build, :278 release needs [build, attest]), and the gate's exit code propagates (no continue-on-error, no set +e; the only || true is on the grep -c at release.yml:72, and an empty count correctly becomes 0 -> exit 2). The R5 exemption is narrow (release.yml:155 exact-equality on the published tag only) and the R2 exemption is safe on a tag push because release.yml:102-112 forces github.ref_name to be in git tag --points-at HEAD, so a branch push can never reach the job. What fails is the operational behaviour. MAJOR 1 - the release pipeline is red today on the tree it is meant to release, and the author's own evidence does not say so. The commit message's Result reports only R1/R2 and the three exit codes. The same checker on the same v1.1.2 tree reports 27 problems: R8 19, R6 6, R2 1, R9 1. Nineteen of them are "PyPI HTTP 404, the channel does not resolve and is unmarked" (README.md:116,119,122, docs/Team/Decisions.md:121, docs/Team/Release.md:116, docs/guide/install.md:108,124,134,167,177,186), because this project deliberately does not publish to PyPI (docs/Team/Release.md:127 "teamwork-agents is not on PyPI") while the README still advertises `pipx install teamwork-agents` / `uvx --from teamwork-agents` / `pip install teamwork-agents` unmarked. The gate correctly refuses to release that; the consequence is that no release can ship until that prose is fixed (ceo T-0061) or the lines are marked with the contract's channel-unavailable marker. Required: state this in the task's self-check/evidence and name the task that clears it, or scope the release gate to the tag rules (R1/R2/R4/R5) and leave the prose rules to ci.yml. Shipping a permanently-red release path without saying so is the defect. MAJOR 2 - the post-publish R5 re-check downgrades an unreadable input to a pass, and it contradicts the pre-publish gate on the same input. Measured: a checker that exits 2 (PyPI 503, i.e. an unreadable input per Architecture.md 11.1.1) while R5 is ok -> the post-publish step exits 0, because release.yml:351-360 inspects only the R5 rule and never looks at proc.returncode. The pre-publish gate does the opposite (release.yml:168-170 turns exit 2 into exit 2). Architecture.md 11.1.1: "An unreadable input is never downgraded to a SKIP that leaves the exit code at 0." Required: either honour the checker's exit code in the post-publish step, or record a decision that the post-publish step deliberately checks R5 only, with evidence, and make the two steps agree. MAJOR 3 - the post-publish R5 re-check has no retry, so GitHub's eventual consistency turns it into a false failure. release.yml:287 creates the Release, release.yml:301-308 then re-checks out and queries the releases API once, release.yml:355-357 turns any R5 DRIFT into exit 1. A freshly created Release that has not yet appeared in the releases listing makes the job red AFTER the release was published, with no bound on retries and no way to distinguish "not yet" from "never". The author flagged PyPI 503s but not this. Required: a bounded retry/backoff around the R5 read (and a clear message) before declaring drift. MAJOR 4 - the pre-publish gate blocks the release on an anonymous GitHub API failure the job could have avoided. Measured twice during this review: "R5 SKIP online: GitHub API unreadable: https://api.github.com/repos/Nidvue/Teamwork/releases: HTTP 403" -> checker exit 2 -> the gate exits 2 -> the release is blocked. The API is anonymous by design (Architecture.md 11.1.4, "no credential and no token from the environment", limit 60/h per IP as measured: x-ratelimit-limit 60), while the release job itself holds a GITHUB_TOKEN with contents: write (release.yml:280). The same 403 is what made the author's own PyPI 503 note a smaller problem than it is. Required: pass the job's GITHUB_TOKEN to the GitHub reads, or record a measured decision that an anonymous 403 blocks the release, plus a retry. MINOR 1 - release.yml:358-360 is dead code: R5 is ok only when the detail is "N tag(s) have a GitHub Release", so the substring test "tag {tag} has no GitHub Release" can never be reached once the status check at :355 passed. It also uses substring matching where the pre-publish gate uses exact equality (:155). Delete it or make it a real assertion. MINOR 2 - release.yml:181-182 propagates the checker's raw exit code (`raise SystemExit(proc.returncode or 1)`), so a non-contract code (3+) escapes as a step failure instead of being mapped to 1/2. Map it explicitly. MINOR 3 - the deliverable depends on a file that is not in the tree and whose task is currently failed. git ls-tree -r 7bcd30e --name-only | grep -c check_release_consistency -> 0; scripts/check_release_consistency.py and release-exceptions.json are untracked in the worktree, and developer T-0005 (which owns the checker) is at review_status=failed. Until it lands, every release exits 2 with "scripts/check_release_consistency.py is missing" (measured: that exact message, exit 2). Add developer:T-0005 to the task's deps. VERIFIED CLEAN (do not redo): (1) the R5 exemption cannot hide a different missing Release - R5 for v1.0.0 -> exit 1, R5 for the published tag together with R5 for v1.0.0 -> exit 1, the exemption at :155 is exact-equality on the published tag only; (2) the R2 exemption is not a hole for a stale HEAD - the job only triggers on `push: tags: v*` (release.yml:18-21) and :102-112 exits 2 unless github.ref_name is among git tag --points-at HEAD, so RELEASE_TAG=main -> exit 2 "main does not point at HEAD", and a stale HEAD naming a non-HEAD tag -> exit 1; (3) exit codes proven in a real clone - checker absent -> 2, real drift -> 1, consistent tree -> 0, and the job has no continue-on-error/set +e/|| true outside the grep -c; (4) the second checkout does not break the release - the publish step reads the artifacts through actions/download-artifact (release.yml:283,287) and the checkout is after it (:301), so nothing needed downstream is clobbered; (5) attest is still correct - it needs build, which now needs consistency, and it needs no tags (it downloads and attests, no checkout); (6) fetch-depth: 0 is present on all three checkouts (:55 consistency, :195 build, :303 release) and a real --depth 1 clone makes the workflow's own assert exit 2; The offline half passes (exit 0, 0 problem(s)) and every other rule is ok (R1/R2/R4/R5/R6/R7/R9 ok, R3 ok '2 npm version(s) match a tagged payload digest'). The two drift lines are the contract's own channel-form sentences in docs/Team/Architecture.md:541-542 ('pip install teamwork-agents ... pipx install teamwork-agents ... uvx --from teamwork-agents'), which carry no <!-- release-consistency: channel-unavailable --> marker; the fix exists and is UNMERGED on architect/t-0004-contract-amendments (f4838f9 'the two lines naming the channel forms carry ...'). Consequence: the 'consistency' job in .github/workflows/release.yml (name 'release consistency gate (online, before anything is published)') runs --online on a v* tag push and would fail, so a v1.1.3 publish is blocked while the tree is in this state. SECOND FINDING: the PR head ceo/t-0059-m9-f-bump-main-to-1-1-3-and-record-the-n = 7fff778 is stale - it does not contain scripts/check_release_consistency.py at all (git merge-base --is-ancestor e4cd4c1 7fff778 -> false; git show 7fff778:docs/Team/Architecture.md has no '## 11.' section), so the required whole-tree checker run cannot even execute on the branch; only main (bdcc588, which contains the checker) satisfies the checks. All other claims in the CHANGELOG 1.1.3 section were verified and hold on main (see the claim table in the Reviewer-3 report).
